It is established through several authentic chains of transmission that the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, said:

“Allah will manifest Himself to His servants at the gathering place when it is said: ‘Let every people follow what they used to worship.’ The polytheists will follow their gods, while the believers will remain. The true Lord will manifest Himself to them in a form other than the one they recognize, and they will not acknowledge Him. Then He will manifest Himself to them in the form they recognize, and the believers will prostrate before Him. The hypocrites’ backs, however, will remain like cattle’s horns: they will wish to prostrate but will be unable to do so. This is what He says: ‘On the Day when a shin is uncovered’—the verse.”

These matters are discussed at length elsewhere.

The point here is that Allah does not torment anyone in the Hereafter except for their own sin, and that no bearer of burdens bears the burden of another. The Prophet’s statement:

“The deceased is tormented by his family’s weeping over him”

does not mean that the woman who wails goes unpunished. Rather, she is punished for her wailing, as stated in

---

## Page 12443

the authentic hadith:

“If a woman who wails does not repent before her death, she will wear a shirt of scabies and a robe of pitch on the Day of Resurrection.”

Thus, no one bears the burden of the person who wails.

As for the deceased being tormented, the Prophet did not say that the deceased is “punished” because of his family’s weeping over him. Rather, he said that he is “tormented.” Torment is broader than punishment: torment is pain, and not everyone who suffers pain because of something is being punished for it. The Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, said:

“Travel is a kind of torment; it deprives you of your food and drink.”

He thus called travel torment, although it is not punishment for a sin. A person is tormented by unpleasant things he perceives, such as terrifying sounds, foul odors, and ugly sights. He suffers from hearing the first, smelling the second, and seeing the third, although none of these is a deed of his for which he is being punished. How, then, can it be denied that the deceased may be tormented by wailing, even though the wailing is not his own deed for which he is being punished?

A person in his grave is tormented by what some people say, and suffers from seeing some of them and hearing their speech. For this reason, the judge Abū Ya‘lā ruled that when sins are committed in the presence of the dead, the dead suffer because of them, as transmitted reports indicate. Thus, their being tormented

---

## Page 12444

by sins committed at their graves is like their being tormented by the wailing of those who mourn them. Wailing is a cause of torment, but the effect of a cause may be prevented by something that counteracts it. The deceased may have been granted such great honor that it wards off torment, just as some people possess enough strength to withstand the harm of terrifying sounds, foul odors, and ugly sights.

The hadiths containing warnings mention the cause, but its consequence may fail to occur because of something that prevents it: accepted repentance, good deeds that erase sins, afflictions that expiate them, the intercession of an intercessor whose plea is accepted, or Allah’s grace, mercy, and forgiveness. For He “does not forgive associating partners with Him, but forgives anything less than that for whomever He wills.”

Whatever pain—which is torment—a believer experiences in this world, in the intermediate life after death, or at the Resurrection, Allah makes it an expiation for his sins. It is established in the two Sahīh collections that the Prophet, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, said:

“No illness, fatigue, anxiety, grief, or harm afflicts a believer—not even the prick of a thorn—without Allah thereby expiating some of his sins.”

In al-Musnad:

“When this verse was revealed: ‘Whoever does evil will be recompensed for it,’ Abū Bakr said, ‘O Messenger of Allah, this is a back-breaking blow! Which of us has not done evil?’

---

## Page 12445

He replied, ‘O Abū Bakr, do you not grieve? Do you not suffer harm?’”

For Paradise is pure, and none but the pure enter it, as Allah Most High says:

“You have become pure, so enter it to abide forever.”

An authentic hadith states:

“When they have crossed the Bridge, they will stop on a bridge between Paradise and Hell, where they will settle the wrongs they have done one another. Once they have been refined and purified, they will be permitted to enter Paradise.”

This issue is discussed at length elsewhere, outside this answer. Allah knows best what is correct.

What we have mentioned—that the dead hear those who address them, that rewards reach them, and that they are tormented by wailing—and even matters the questioner did not ask about, such as their punishment in their graves and other matters, may be disclosed to many of our contemporaries, whether awake or in dreams. They come to know these things and ascertain their reality. We have many instances of this. In answering questions of religious knowledge, however, one relies on what the Quran and Sunnah convey, for everyone is obliged to affirm its truth.

Whatever a person has had disclosed to him concerning these matters, or has been told by someone he considers truthful, benefits him if he knows it, increasing his faith in, and affirmation of, what the scriptural texts convey. Yet humanity as a whole is not obliged to believe anything beyond what the prophets have brought. Allah, Mighty and Majestic, has required belief in what the prophets brought, as in His words:

“Say, ‘We believe in Allah’”—and the rest of the verse.

Allah Most High also says:

“Rather, the truly righteous are those who believe in Allah, the Last Day, the angels, the Scripture, and the prophets”—and the rest of the verse.

It is established in the two Sahīh collections that the Prophet

---

## Page 12446

, may Allah bless him and grant him peace, said:

“Among the nations before you there were people who received inspiration. If there is anyone of this kind in my nation, it is ‘Umar.”

Anyone in this nation who receives inspiration and has matters disclosed to him must weigh these against the Quran and Sunnah. If they accord with them, he accepts what has come to him as true; if they contradict them, he pays it no heed. This obligation applied even to ‘Umar, may Allah be pleased with him, the foremost of those who received inspiration: if something was cast into his heart that contradicted the Sunnah, it was not to be accepted from him. For he was not infallible; infallibility belongs only to prophethood.

This is why al-Siddīq was superior to ‘Umar. Al-Siddīq draws not from his own heart but from the light of prophethood, which is protected from error. A person who receives inspiration, however, draws sometimes from his heart and sometimes from prophethood. Whatever he receives from prophethood is protected from error and must be followed. As for what is inspired in his heart, if it accords with what prophethood has brought, it is true; if it contradicts it, it is false.

For this reason, people of knowledge and faith rely, in such matters of religious knowledge, only on the texts of the Quran and Sunnah and the consensus of the Muslim community. They may nevertheless possess corroboration and evidence concerning some of these matters through what they have witnessed and experienced, and through what they have understood and practised. This benefits them personally. Allah Most High’s proof against His servants, however, is His messengers. Beyond this, these matters contain rational proofs and considerations, as well as corroborating evidence

---

## Page 12447

from sensory experience and spiritual disclosure that benefit those who experience them. Human reasoning and spiritual disclosures are subordinate to what the messengers brought from Allah Most High. Whatever is true in them agrees with what the messengers brought from Allah Most High and does not contradict it. Yet even when true, it does not settle disagreements among people, nor must those who have not experienced it affirm it as they must affirm what is known to be protected from error: the words of the prophets, may Allah’s blessings and peace be upon them. Nevertheless, whoever gains insight, rational understanding, or demonstrative proof concerning such matters has light upon light.

One of the early generations said:

“The believer’s insight speaks wisdom, even if he has heard no transmitted report concerning it. When the transmitted report comes, it is light upon light.”

“And whoever Allah does not grant light has no light.”

Allah Most High said:

“Mankind was one community; then Allah sent the prophets as bearers of good tidings and warnings, and sent down with them the Scripture in truth to judge between people concerning their differences. None differed over it except those who had been given it, after clear proofs had come to them, out of mutual rivalry. Then Allah, by His permission, guided those who believed to the truth concerning which they had differed. And Allah guides whom He wills to a straight path.”

---

## Page 12448

He—may Allah have mercy on him—was also asked:

“Does the deceased speak in his grave or not?”

He replied:

He speaks, and he may also hear those who speak to him, as is authentically reported from the Prophet, may Allah’s blessings and peace be upon him, who said:

“They hear the sound of their sandals.”

It is also authentically reported from him:

“The deceased is questioned in his grave. He is asked, ‘Who is your Lord? What is your religion? Who is your prophet?’ Allah keeps the believer firm with the firm word, and he says, ‘Allah is my Lord, Islam is my religion, and Muhammad is my prophet.’ He is also asked, ‘What do you say about this man who was sent among you?’ The believer replies, ‘He is Allah’s servant and Messenger. He brought us clear proofs and guidance, so we believed in him and followed him.’”

This is the meaning of the words of Allah Most High:

“Allah keeps those who believe firm with the firm word, in the life of this world and in the Hereafter.”

It is authentically reported from the Prophet, may Allah’s blessings and peace be upon him, that this verse was revealed concerning the punishment of the grave.

The hypocrite likewise speaks, saying, “Ah, ah! I do not know. I heard people saying something, so I said it too.” He is then struck with an iron mallet and lets out a scream that everything except human beings can hear.

---

## Page 12449

It is also established in the Sahīh that he said:

“Were it not that you might cease burying one another, I would ask Allah to let you hear something of the punishment of the grave, as I hear it.”

It is also established in the Sahīh that he called out to the polytheists on the day of Badr, after he had them thrown into the well, and said:

“You do not hear what I say any better than they do.”

The reports concerning this are numerous and widespread. Allah knows best.

He was asked:

“Does a mother’s and brothers’ weeping over the deceased cause him any harm?”

He replied:

There is no sin in tears shed by the eyes or sorrow in the heart. However, lamentation and wailing are forbidden. Any charity given on behalf of the deceased benefits him.

He was asked about matters relating to offering condolences.

He replied:

Offering condolences is recommended. It is reported in al-Tirmidhī that the Prophet, may Allah’s blessings and peace be upon him, said:

“Whoever consoles someone afflicted by calamity will have a reward like his.”

As for the words some people say:

---

## Page 12450

“May whatever was taken from his lifespan be added to yours”—this is not recommended. Rather, one should offer a beneficial supplication for the bereaved, such as:

“May Allah increase your reward, grant you comfort in your loss, and forgive your deceased.”

As for the shortening and lengthening of lifespans, some people maintain that this cannot happen under any circumstances, interpreting the reports on this matter as referring to increased blessing. The correct view, however, is that a lifespan may be shortened or lengthened relative to what is recorded in the angels’ scrolls. Allah’s eternal knowledge, however, does not change.

As for the Preserved Tablet, there are two views on whether its contents may be changed. On this understanding, the scriptural texts concerning this subject are reconciled.

Preparing food for the family of the deceased is recommended, as the Prophet, may Allah’s blessings and peace be upon him, said:

“Prepare food for Jaʿfar’s family, for something has befallen them that has occupied their attention.”

However, such food is lawful only when the giver offers it willingly and it is given in reciprocation, such as in return for a comparable kindness. If a person knows that it is not lawful, he must not eat any of it. If its status is uncertain, there is no harm in eating a little when doing so serves an overriding benefit, such as fostering affection between people or something similar. Allah knows best.

---

## Page 12451

He was asked:

About someone who recites the Quran, wails at a grave, and says inappropriate things, while women have their faces uncovered and men are gathered around them.

He replied:

Praise be to Allah. Wailing is forbidden for both men and women according to the well-known imams. It is authentically reported from the Prophet, may Allah’s blessings and peace be upon him:

“A woman who wails, if she does not repent before her death, will wear on the Day of Resurrection a coat of scabies and a garment of tar.”

The Sunan collections also report from him:

“He cursed the woman who wails and the woman who listens to her.”

It is also authentically reported that he said:

“He is not one of us who slaps his cheeks, tears open his neckline, and utters the cries of the pre-Islamic age of ignorance.”

Women uncovering their faces where non-mahram men can see them is not permissible. Those in authority must enjoin what is right and forbid this and other wrongdoing. Anyone who does not desist must be punished in a manner that deters them. This applies especially to women’s wailing at graves, for this involves sins that Allah and His Messenger detest—such as impatience in grief

---

## Page 12452

and lamentation, wailing, distressing the deceased, exposing the living to temptation, consuming people’s wealth unjustly, failing to show patience and seek Allah’s reward as Allah and His Messenger have commanded, doing things that lead to indecency, and opening the door to it—all of which Muslims must forbid. And Allah knows best. May Allah bless Muhammad, his family, and his Companions, and grant them peace.

> End of Volume Twenty-Four
